* About this document

This document is the design of LUNA and the source of the phase 1 build.
LUNA is a private personal assistant that runs all the time on a VPS.
You talk to LUNA from your phone and from your laptop.

This file is in the nyx flake at =luna/LUNA.org=.
The VPS is the nyx host =luna=.
The laptop =beta= and the VPS =luna= share one flake and one =flake.lock=.

Tangle this file to make the build files.
In Emacs, use =C-c C-v t=.
From a shell, in =nyx/luna=, run =make tangle=.
Edit this file, not the tangled files.
The next tangle overwrites the tangled files.

| Tangled file         | Content                              |
|----------------------+--------------------------------------|
| =hosts/luna/*.nix=   | The host: site values, system, disk  |
| =modules/luna/*.nix= | Hardening, access, Matrix, the agent |
| =pkgs/luna-core/=    | The agent package and its tests      |
| =pkgs/luna-web/=     | The Phoenix web app and its tests    |
| =pkgs/luna-core-ex/= | The Elixir core and its tests (E5)   |
| =luna/memory-seed/=  | The first memory files               |
| =luna/tailscale/=    | The Tailscale policy                 |
| =luna/tests/=        | The VM test of the web app           |
| =luna/Makefile=      | The install and deploy commands      |

Two nyx files are not tangled.
Edit =flake.nix= and =.sops.yaml= by hand.
Refer to [[*nyx integration][nyx integration]].

* Mission

LUNA has one mission: help Coops move from survival to freedom.
Every phase serves one or more of these five goals.

1. *Annual living costs.* Plan all living costs for the next 12 months.
   Coops thinks about money once a year, not once a week.
2. *A spend amount.* Give one number each week: the safe amount to spend.
3. *Good off-swings.* Plan and book each two-week break in the FIFO roster.
   The places are interesting, cheap, clean and comfortable.
4. *Project focus.* Score the software projects.
   Keep one project in the "ship" stage until it earns money.
5. *Compounding.* Move the surplus to savings and investments by a written plan.

** What LUNA does not do

- LUNA does not send money, buy, book or send email without approval.
  The approval mechanism is code, not a model decision.
  Refer to [[*Approvals][Approvals]].
- LUNA does not promise wealth.
  Income comes from work and from products that customers pay for.
  LUNA makes the work faster and the money visible.

* Principles

1. *Advise and prepare. Coops approves.*
   An action that moves money, spends money or speaks for Coops needs approval.
2. *Assume that LUNA will be fooled.*
   Email, web pages and documents can contain hidden instructions.
   The design must stay safe when the model follows such an instruction.
3. *No public attack surface.*
   All services listen on the tailnet or on localhost.
4. *Declarative and reproducible.*
   The nyx flake describes the full server.
   A rebuild from zero takes less than one hour.
5. *Plain text memory.*
   LUNA keeps its memory in org-mode files in git.
   Coops can read, edit and revert all memory in Emacs.
6. *Record everything.*
   LUNA writes each tool call to an append-only audit log.

* Threat model

| Threat                                      | Control                                                          |
|---------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Prompt injection in email, web or documents | Tiered tools. Tier 2 needs approval in code. Split agents later. |
| Attack on public ports                      | No public ports after bootstrap. Tailscale only.                 |
| Theft of the laptop or the phone            | Remove the device in the Tailscale admin console.                |
| Leak of API keys                            | sops-nix. systemd credentials. Scoped, read-only keys.           |
| A compromised VPS                           | Tailscale ACL: the VPS cannot connect to your devices.           |
| A bad model action                          | Audit log. Memory in git. Approvals expire after 24 hours.       |
| Lockout                                     | Two devices. Vultr console. Recovery sheet. Refer to Access.     |
| A malicious package update                  | Pinned flake inputs. Update on purpose with =make update=.       |

** The prompt injection rule

An agent that reads untrusted text must not hold a tool that can do damage.
In phase 1, LUNA reads web pages and its own memory.
LUNA has no tier 2 tool in phase 1.
The inbox phase adds a separate reader agent.
The reader agent has no write tools.
The reader agent gives a structured summary to the main agent.

* Tools and tiers

| Tier | Meaning                   | Phase 1 tools                                     | Approval             |
|------+---------------------------+---------------------------------------------------+----------------------|
|    0 | Read                      | memory_search, memory_read, web_search, web_fetch | No                   |
|    1 | Write, and easy to revert | memory_append (git commit)                        | No                   |
|    2 | Act outside LUNA          | (none in phase 1)                                 | Yes, always, in code |

** Approvals

A tier 2 tool does not do the action.
The tool writes an approval request to disk and returns the request ID.
LUNA sends the request to Coops in Matrix.
Coops replies =approve <id>= or =deny <id>=.
The bot parses this reply with plain code.
The model never sees an approval as a tool result that it can make.
The bot accepts approvals only from the owner account.
A request expires after 24 hours.

* Access without a hardware key

You do not use a YubiKey.
These controls give strong access without one more key to lose.

1. *Tailscale identity.*
   Log in to Tailscale with an account that has a passkey or TOTP.
   Put the recovery codes in your password manager.
2. *Two devices.*
   Join the laptop and the phone to the tailnet.
   If you lose one device, use the other device to remove it.
3. *Tailscale SSH.*
   Tailscale SSH uses your tailnet identity.
   You do not copy SSH keys to the phone.
4. *Break glass.*
   The Vultr web console gives a local login.
   The owner password is in sops and in your password manager.
   Protect the Vultr account with TOTP.
5. *The password manager is the root of trust.*
   Keep in it: the Vultr TOTP recovery codes, the Tailscale recovery codes,
   the owner password, the sops admin age key and the Matrix password.
6. *Recovery sheet.*
   Print one page with the password manager recovery kit.
   Keep it in your bag, not on a device.

* Architecture

#+begin_example
  Phone (Element X) ──┐                       ┌── Telegram (nudges only, optional)
  Laptop (ement.el) ──┤ WireGuard (Tailscale) │
                      ▼                       │
  ┌───────────── Vultr VPS: NixOS ────────────┴────────────────────┐
  │ tailscale serve :443 ──► Synapse (127.0.0.1:8008, no federation)│
  │                              ▲                                  │
  │                              │ Matrix client API                │
  │ luna-core (systemd, sandboxed user "luna")                      │
  │   ├─ matrix bot ── owner-only, approval commands in code        │
  │   ├─ agent ────── Claude API, tool runner, tier 0/1 tools       │
  │   ├─ scheduler ── morning brief, memory sync                    │
  │   ├─ approvals ── /var/lib/luna/approvals                       │
  │   ├─ audit ────── /var/lib/luna/audit/audit.jsonl (append-only) │
  │   └─ memory ───── /var/lib/luna/memory  ⇄  memory.git (hub)     │
  │                                              ▲                  │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┘
                                                 │ git over Tailscale SSH
                                         Laptop: ~/org/luna (Emacs)
#+end_example

** Why Matrix first, and Telegram only for nudges

Telegram bot chats are not end-to-end encrypted.
Telegram stores the text of each bot message.
Thus LUNA sends no personal data to Telegram.
A Telegram nudge says only that LUNA needs you.

The Matrix homeserver runs on the VPS.
The homeserver has no federation and no public port.
The traffic goes through WireGuard and TLS.
The Matrix room is not end-to-end encrypted.
The bot keys would live on the same server, so encryption adds little here.

Clients:
- Phone: Element X.
- Laptop: =ement.el= in Emacs, or Element desktop.

** Server size

The Vultr plan =vx1-g-4c-16g-240s= has 4 vCPU, 16 GB RAM and 240 GB disk.
LUNA uses the Claude API, so the server does not run a local model.
The plan has space for Synapse, luna-core, CI jobs and build caches.

* Roadmap

| Phase | Name               | Goal served | Risk   | Status   |
|-------+--------------------+-------------+--------+----------|
|     1 | Foundation         |         All | Low    | This doc |
|     2 | Money: annual plan |     1, 2, 5 | Low    | Spec     |
|     3 | Off-swing travel   |           3 | Medium | Spec     |
|     4 | Project portfolio  |           4 | Low    | Spec     |
|     5 | Inbox              |         All | High   | Spec     |
|     6 | Dev partner        |           4 | Medium | Spec     |
|     7 | Hardware inventory |           4 | Low    | Spec     |
|     8 | Investments        |           5 | High   | Spec     |

** Phase 1: Foundation

Acceptance tests:
1. The VPS has no open public port, except the Tailscale UDP port.
2. Element X on the phone logs in to the homeserver.
3. LUNA replies in Matrix on the phone and on the laptop.
4. LUNA ignores messages from accounts that are not the owner.
5. =memory_append= makes a git commit in the hub.
6. =git pull= on the laptop gets the commit.
7. Each tool call has a line in the audit log.
8. The morning brief arrives at the set time.

** Phase 2: Money — the annual plan

*Data in.*
LUNA starts with CSV exports.
CSV exports need no bank credentials on the server.
- Bank accounts: CSV export from each bank.
- Wise: a personal API token with read-only access, or CSV statements.
- CommSec: the holdings and transactions CSV.
- Crypto exchanges: API keys with read-only access. Withdrawals disabled.
- Later option: an accredited Consumer Data Right (CDR) data provider.

*Ledger.*
LUNA keeps the ledger in hledger format in =money/main.journal=.
Periodic transactions describe the next 12 months.
The command =hledger forecast= shows the year ahead.
Coops reads the same ledger in Emacs with =ledger-mode= or =hledger-mode=.

*Account buckets.* LUNA proposes this structure for the empty accounts.

| Bucket | Purpose                                  | Fed by                       |
|--------+------------------------------------------+------------------------------|
| Income | Pay lands here                           | Employer                     |
| Bills  | All annual and regular costs, pre-funded | Fixed transfer each pay      |
| Buffer | Three months of costs, then stop         | Fixed transfer until full    |
| Spend  | The safe-to-spend amount                 | Transfer each pay            |
| Invest | Long-term investments                    | All surplus after the buffer |

*The safe-to-spend formula.*

#+begin_example
spend_per_week = (pay_per_year
                  - annual_costs
                  - buffer_contribution_per_year
                  - invest_target_per_year) / 52
#+end_example

LUNA posts this number every Monday.
LUNA also posts the current week's spend against the number.

*The annual cost table* is in =money.org=.
Each row is one cost with an amount, a due date and a bucket.
LUNA finds costs in the CSV exports that are not in the table.

*Base or hotels.*
A 2/2 roster gives about 182 nights off each year.
LUNA calculates the yearly cost of hotels against a rented base.
LUNA includes flights, food and the cost of a kitchen against takeaway food.

** Phase 3: Off-swing travel

- =roster.org= holds the swing dates for 12 months.
- =travel.org= holds the preferences: price per night, clean, a kitchen,
  a gym near, quiet, interesting, near the site airport.
- For each off-swing, LUNA proposes three options with prices.
- LUNA watches fares and alerts when a price drops.
- A booking is a tier 2 action.
  LUNA books only after =approve <id>=.
- Data sources: a flight API (for example Duffel) and an accommodation API.

** Phase 4: Project portfolio

- =projects.org= holds one row per project and a score.
- The score favours a short time to the first paying customer.
- Exactly one project has the stage =SHIP=.
- The weekly review asks one question: what did the =SHIP= project ship?

** Phase 5: Inbox

- A separate reader agent reads email with a read-only scope.
- The reader agent has no tools that write or send.
- The reader agent gives a structured summary: sender, intent, deadline, action.
- The main agent drafts replies. Sending is tier 2.

*Payslips.* Payslips arrive by email.
- The reader agent finds the payslip email.
- Code extracts the text from the PDF on luna. The model does not read the PDF.
- Code keeps the pay date, gross, tax, super and net pay only.
- Code removes the TFN, the address and the employee ID before the model sees any text.
- LUNA writes the figures to =money.org= and to the ledger. LUNA does not keep the PDF.

*Account data.* LUNA uses read-only access only:
bank CSV exports, and the read-only personal API tokens of Up and Wise.
LUNA never holds a bank login. A shared login can void the protection of the ePayments Code.

** Phase 6: Dev partner

- LUNA works in git worktrees in a sandboxed user on the VPS.
- LUNA opens pull requests. LUNA never pushes to the main branch.
- CI runs on the VPS in a separate systemd slice with resource limits.

** Phase 7: Hardware inventory

- =inventory.org= holds parts, quantity, location and supplier part numbers.
- LUNA makes a bill of materials from a project and marks the missing parts.
- LUNA compares prices across suppliers. A purchase is tier 2.

** Phase 8: Investments

- Read-only data: CommSec holdings, exchange balances, Wise balances.
- A monthly net worth report.
- Rebalance alerts against a written plan in =money.org=.
- No automatic trades. A trade is tier 2 with a hard limit per trade.

* Security review before phase 2

The review ran on 2026-11-20, on the live server, before any money data.

** Controls in place

| Control                                   | Evidence                                              |
|-------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------|
| Only Tailscale UDP 41641 is public        | =ss -tulpn=, firewall rules, refused packet counters  |
| The VPS cannot connect to the laptop      | A TCP test from luna to beta failed                   |
| Strict systemd sandbox                    | =systemd-analyze security=: luna-core 1.4, Synapse 1.1 |
| Secrets are root-only, not in the store   | =/run/secrets/*= has mode =r--------=                 |
| The audit log is append-only              | =lsattr= shows the =a= attribute                      |
| The laptop disk is encrypted              | beta root is on LUKS                                  |

** Findings and fixes

| ID  | Finding                                                    | Fix                                                         | Owner |
|-----+------------------------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+-------|
| F1  | A Matrix token was in a chat log. A registration session was live. | Sign out both sessions in Element. Reconnect ement.  | Coops |
| F2  | One conversation can read money data, read web text and send requests out | Taint: a money read removes the web tools | LUNA  |
| F3  | Transaction text goes to the Claude API                    | Totals by default. The import masks account numbers.       | LUNA  |
| F4  | A stolen API key can spend without a limit                 | Set a monthly spend limit in a LUNA-only workspace          | Coops |
| F5  | The VPS disk is not encrypted                              | Raw exports go to an inbox outside git. The hub refuses them. The import deletes them. | LUNA |
| F6  | The memory has no backup                                   | An hourly pull to the encrypted laptop (=home/luna.nix=)    | LUNA  |
| F7  | Updates depend on memory                                   | The brief warns when nixpkgs is older than 14 days          | LUNA  |
| F8  | A Tailscale control plane compromise can add a node        | Enable Tailnet Lock with keys on the laptop and the phone   | Coops |
| F9  | Account takeover                                           | TOTP or a passkey on Vultr, Tailscale, the Claude Console and the git host. The nyx remote is private. | Coops |
| F10 | sshd is not necessary after the bootstrap                  | sshd runs during the bootstrap only                         | LUNA  |

** The gate for phase 2

#+begin_quote
*CAUTION:* Do not put money data on luna before this test passes.
#+end_quote

F2 uses a =tool_removal= system message under the beta =mid-conversation-tool-changes-2026-07-01=.
The documentation does not say that a removal stops a server tool such as =web_search=.
The test asks the model to read a secret and then search the web for it.
The test passes when the API runs no search after the removal.

1. Add credits to the Claude Console account of LUNA.
2. Run the test on luna. The test is =scratchpad/test-tool-removal.py= in the session that wrote this review.
3. Make sure that the output says =RESULT: PASS=.
4. Write the date of the pass here.
5. On luna, replace =money/main.journal= with the seed version.
   The first seed had no =include tx/*.journal= line. The money tools refuse an old file.
6. Set =money = true= in the site values. Deploy.

Status: *not run*. The first run failed with "credit balance is too low".

* LUNA on Elixir

This section is the plan to move LUNA to the stack of Coops:
Elixir, Phoenix, JavaScript, Go and PostgreSQL.
The move has stages. Each stage keeps LUNA in service.
The Python core stays until the new core passes the same tests.

** Target architecture

#+begin_example
  Browser (laptop, phone)                  Element X / ement.el
        │ HTTPS :8443, Tailscale identity        │ Matrix
        ▼                                        ▼
  ┌──────────── luna (NixOS) ───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ luna_web: Phoenix LiveView ─── PostgreSQL (unix socket, peer auth)│
  │   ├─ Chat, task view, widgets, approvals, money, system          │
  │   ├─ Camera frames, push-to-talk, voice replies                  │
  │   └─ Wyoming client ──► faster-whisper (STT)  127.0.0.1:10300    │
  │                    └──► piper (TTS, female voice) 127.0.0.1:10200│
  │        │ internal API, unix socket                               │
  │        ▼                                                         │
  │ luna-core (Python now; Elixir or Go later): agent, tools, Matrix  │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
#+end_example

** Decisions

| Item              | Decision                                                            |
|-------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Web interface     | Phoenix LiveView. No JavaScript framework. Small hooks for camera and audio. |
| Database          | PostgreSQL 18 on luna. Unix socket and peer authentication only.    |
| Audit log         | A PostgreSQL table. A trigger refuses UPDATE and DELETE.            |
| Login             | A Plug checks =Tailscale-User-Login=, as the Python app does.       |
| Voice in          | =services.wyoming.faster-whisper=, local on luna                    |
| Voice out         | =services.wyoming.piper=, local on luna, a female voice             |
| Packaging         | =mixRelease= with =fetchMixDeps= in the nyx flake                   |
| Core language     | Elixir, with Req for HTTP. Coops decided on 2026-11-20.            |
| Third parties     | As few as possible. Build a tool when that is better than a subscription. |
| Money ledger      | Stays hledger plain text in the memory, for Emacs                   |
| Clients           | The laptop and the iPhone now. An iPhone app and a watch app later. |
| Wake word         | "LUNA". Refer to [[*Wake word][Wake word]].                                    |
| Presence          | Orb, ring or avatar. Each device can switch. Refer to [[*Presence][Presence]]. |
| Persona           | Smart, sassy, succinct, loyal. Refer to [[*Persona][Persona]].                 |
| Long-term memory  | One org-roam graph, shared by Coops and LUNA. Refer to [[*Long-term memory][Long-term memory]]. |

** Stages

| Stage | Result                                                                    | Done when                          |
|-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------|
| E1    | Phoenix app and PostgreSQL in the flake. Deploy at :8443 in place of the Python app. | The page loads with the Tailscale login |
| E2    | Chat, approvals, money and system panels in LiveView, through the core API | All Python web tests pass as Elixir tests |
| E3    | Voice: push-to-talk, faster-whisper, Piper female voice. Presence: orb and ring. | A spoken question gets a spoken reply |
| E4    | Task view (plan, tool calls, results) and fixed widgets                   | The core streams events to the page |
| E5    | Port the core. Port the tests first.                                       | All core tests pass. Python retires. |
| E6    | The iPhone app, then the watch app. Wake word, earbuds and notifications.  | "LUNA" wakes the phone in a pocket |

The build of E1 and E2 is in [[*Build: the Phoenix app][Build: the Phoenix app]].
The contract between the page and the core is in [[*Build: the core API][Build: the core API]].

A second review on the same day looked at the interface only: voice, presence and the ambient display.
Its notes and the candidate items 13 to 22 are in [[file:research/interface-review.org][research/interface-review.org]].
The research for items 23 to 26 is in [[file:research/local-models.org][research/local-models.org]].
Each idea has its source project and its stage.

|----+-------------------------------------------------------------------------+-------------------+-------|
| 1  | Approve with a Matrix reaction. Code still parses and checks the reply.  | OpenClaw          | core  |
| 2  | Answer fixed phrases ("safe to spend", "next swing") in code first      | Home Assistant, OVOS | E5 |
| 3  | A spoken morning brief for the FIFO roster                              | OpenJarvis        | E6    |
| 4  | Voice through the Wyoming protocol (E3), with a local wake word (E6)   | Home Assistant    | E3, E6 |
| 5  | Voice input cannot approve a tier 2 action                              | Home Assistant    | E3    |
| 6  | A task view: the plan, each tool call and each result, live            | Leon              | E4    |
| 7  | Fixed widgets in chat: safe-to-spend gauge, bills, roster strip         | Leon, LibreChat   | E4    |
| 8  | Labelled memory sections. Some sections are read-only to LUNA.          | Letta             | E5    |
| 9  | Scheduled jobs in plain language, with the result in Matrix             | Hermes, Khoj      | E5    |
| 10 | Refuse a tier 2 request from a scheduled job when no human is present (done: the brief has no payment tool) | OpenClaw | E5 |
| 11 | Transcribe voice memos from Matrix                                       | Hermes            | E5    |
| 12 | Branch a conversation to compare plans                                  | LibreChat         | E4    |
| 23 | Model profiles: cloud, local and local uncensored (abliterated). Switch by voice, button or =/model=. Code switches, not the model. | Coops | E5 |
| 24 | llama.cpp server on luna. Model files pinned by hash. Measure with =llama-bench= first. | Coops | E5 |
| 25 | The presence element shows the active profile                            | Coops             | E4    |
| 26 | A mission-control System panel: profiles, memory, tools, costs          | Jack Roberts      | E4    |

** Ideas that LUNA does not use

- Control of a computer or a shell. One command can send data out after a money read.
- Plugin marketplaces. A plugin runs code from a stranger inside LUNA.
- Approval by a model. Code checks each approval. The model never approves itself.
- Skills that LUNA writes and uses at once. A new skill is a git diff that Coops reviews.
- Many public chat channels. Each channel adds attack surface outside the tailnet.
- Panels made from HTML that the model writes. That is an injection risk.

* Persona and presence

This section holds the decisions of Coops on 2026-11-20 about the character
of LUNA and the devices. The interface review is in
[[file:research/interface-review.org][research/interface-review.org]].

** Persona

LUNA is smart, sassy, succinct and loyal.
The text below goes in the system prompt of the agent, through the noweb
reference =<<luna-persona>>=. The system prompt is code, so LUNA can read the
persona but cannot change it. Change the persona here, then tangle.

#+name: luna-persona
#+begin_src text :tangle no
Who you are:
- Smart. Think before you answer. Notice what Coops missed, and say it.
- Sassy. You have a dry, quick wit. Tease Coops when he earns it: a bad bet,
  a forgotten bill, a fourth new project. Never cruel. Never about a thing
  that he cannot change. Drop the wit when he is tired, at site, or when the
  news is bad.
- Succinct. The answer comes first, in one or two sentences. Give detail only
  when he asks, or when the numbers need it. No filler. No praise of the
  question. No summary of what you just said.
- Loyal. You are on the side of Coops, so you tell him the truth. Push back
  once, clearly, on a choice that hurts the mission. Then respect his decision.
  Keep his private matters private. Never pretend to agree.
#+end_src

** Devices

| Device          | Now                                   | Later (stage E6)                      |
|-----------------+---------------------------------------+---------------------------------------|
| Laptop          | The page in the browser               | The same                              |
| iPhone          | The page in Safari, with earbuds      | An iPhone app                         |
| Smart watch     | None                                  | A watch app, through the iPhone app   |

A web page on the iPhone has limits:
- Safari stops the microphone when the screen locks or the page goes to the background.
- A page cannot listen for a wake word in a pocket.
- A page cannot read the buttons of the earbuds.
- A watch app needs a native iPhone app.

Thus stage E3 gives push-to-talk on the page, with the audio in the earbuds.
Stage E6 gives the wake word in a pocket, the earbuds buttons and the watch.
The iPhone app is one more client of the same state machine and the same
events. The page and the app must not need different data from the core.

** Wake word

The wake word is "LUNA".
A short word gives more false wakes than a long word.
The model and the voice check together decide a wake. Refer to finding 5 of
the interface review.
Before stage E6, record some days of normal sound on the phone.
Count the false wakes. If there are too many, use "Hey LUNA" in place of "LUNA".
Train the model with the openWakeWord tools. Do not use a stock model.

** Presence

The presence element shows the state of LUNA: =idle=, =listening=,
=thinking=, =working=, =speaking=, =error=.
There are three styles: orb, ring and avatar.
Each device keeps its own choice, so the watch can use the ring and the
laptop can use the avatar.
All three styles use the same states and the same loudness signal from the
voice of LUNA. A new style needs no change in the core.

The avatar is a VRM model, drawn with three.js and three-vrm.
The page loads both libraries from the app, not from the internet.
The avatar model must be an original model, made for LUNA.
Do not use a model of a character from a film, a game or a brand.

* Install procedure

#+begin_quote
*WARNING:* The install erases the disk of the VPS.
Use a new VPS that has no data on it.
#+end_quote

** On the laptop

Do these steps in =nyx/luna=.

1. Copy the public key into the repo:
   =cp ~/.ssh/luna_ed25519.pub keys/=.
   Edit [[*Site values][Site values]] if you need to change other values.
2. Tangle this file: =make tangle=.
3. Make the host key: =make host-key=.
4. In =nyx/.sops.yaml=, replace the =&luna= placeholder with the host key.
   The admin key is the =&coops= key that nyx uses now.
   Put a copy of the =&coops= private key in your password manager.
5. Make the secrets file: =make secrets=.
   Refer to [[*Secrets][Secrets]] for the keys to write.
6. Add the new files to git: =git add -A= in =nyx=.
   The flake does not see files that git does not track.
7. Run the checks: =make check=.

** On Vultr

1. Deploy a =vx1-g-4c-16g-240s= instance with Debian or Ubuntu.
2. Add your laptop SSH key to the instance.
3. Write down the public IP address.

** Install and start

1. Run =make install VPS_IP=<address>=.
2. Delete the local host key: =make forget-host-key=.
3. In the Tailscale admin console, enable MagicDNS and HTTPS certificates.
4. Apply the Tailscale policy from [[*Tailscale policy][Tailscale policy]].
5. Make sure that =ssh root@luna= works over the tailnet.
6. Make the Matrix accounts: =make matrix-users=.
7. Set =bootstrap = false= in the site values.
8. Deploy: =make deploy=.
   This step closes the public SSH port.
9. On the phone, install Element X.
   Log in to =https://luna.<tailnet>= as the owner.
10. Start a direct chat with =@luna:luna.<tailnet>=.
11. On the laptop, clone the memory:
    =git clone coops@luna:/var/lib/luna/memory.git ~/org/luna=.

** Secrets

Write these keys in =secrets/luna.yaml= with =make secrets=.

| Key                        | Value                                                    |
|----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------|
| anthropic-api-key          | An API key from the Claude Console                       |
| tailscale-authkey          | A one-off, pre-approved auth key with the tag =tag:luna= |
| coops-password             | The output of =mkpasswd -m yescrypt=                     |
| matrix-registration-secret | A random string: =openssl rand -hex 32=                  |
| luna-matrix-password       | A random string: =openssl rand -hex 32=                  |
| wise-api-token             | Optional. Only if =wise.mode= is =draft= or =auto=      |
| telegram-bot-token         | Optional. Only if =telegram = true=                      |
| telegram-chat-id           | Optional. Only if =telegram = true=                      |

* Maintenance

| Task                    | Command                   | When           |
|-------------------------+---------------------------+----------------|
| Update the flake inputs | =nix flake update= in nyx | Each week      |
| Deploy a change         | =make deploy=             | After a change |
| Read the audit log      | =make audit=              | When needed    |
| Read the service log    | =make logs=               | When needed    |

=nix flake update= updates the laptop and the VPS together.
After an update, run =make check= here and rebuild =beta=.

* Build: the host
** Site values

Edit these values before the first install.

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../hosts/luna/site.nix
# The host name (luna) and the owner (coops) come from mkHost in flake.nix.
{
  # The MagicDNS suffix of your tailnet. Find it in the Tailscale admin console.
  tailnet = "bumblebee-tuatara.ts.net";

  # The SSH public keys that can log in. Tailscale SSH serves the phone.
  # luna/keys/luna_ed25519.pub is a copy of ~/.ssh/luna_ed25519.pub.
  # The private key stays in ~/.ssh and never goes in the repo.
  sshKeys = [
    (builtins.readFile ../../luna/keys/luna_ed25519.pub)
  ];

  # The Tailscale login of the owner. Only this login can open the web app.
  ownerLogin = "cooper@oscarfono.com";

  # The server clock uses UTC. LUNA uses your time zone for briefs and dates.
  timeZone = "UTC";
  ownerTimeZone = "Australia/Perth";

  # The time of the morning brief, in the owner time zone (HH:MM).
  briefTime = "07:00";

  # Set to true for the install. Set to false after Tailscale works.
  # true opens port 22 to the public internet.
  bootstrap = false;

  # Set to true to send nudges to Telegram. Nudges contain no personal data.
  telegram = false;

  # The core that runs as luna-core: "elixir" (stage E5) or "python".
  # To go back to the Python core, set "python" and run make deploy.
  core = "elixir";

  # CAUTION: keep false until the gate test in "The gate for phase 2" passes.
  # true gives the agent the money tools and starts the Monday money post.
  money = false;

  # The voice of LUNA. Refer to "Voice" in LUNA.org.
  # sttModel: base-int8 is faster, small-int8 is more accurate. Measure on luna.
  # ttsVoice: a Piper voice name. lengthScale above 1.0 speaks slower.
  voice = {
    # base-int8: 1.85 s for 2.7 s of speech on luna. small-int8: 4.4 s.
    sttModel = "base-int8";
    ttsVoice = "en_GB-cori-high";
    lengthScale = 1.0;
  };

  # The Wise float. Refer to "Build: the Wise float" in LUNA.org.
  # WARNING: a payment cannot be undone. Keep mode = "dry-run" until the
  # approval flow works for some weeks. Modes: off, dry-run, draft, auto.
  # draft and auto need the secret wise-api-token. auto needs Wise Business.
  wise = {
    mode = "off";
    sandbox = true;          # api.wise-sandbox.com. Set false for real money.
    profileType = "personal";
    maxPerPayment = 100;     # in the payee currency
    maxPerDay = 200;
    # One entry for each payee. LUNA can pay these payees only.
    # landlord = { recipientId = 12345678; currency = "AUD"; maxPerPayment = 80; };
    payees = { };
  };
}
#+end_src

** nyx integration

LUNA uses the nyx =nixpkgs= (nixos-unstable).
The agent needs the new Anthropic SDK, and nixos-unstable has the SDK.
LUNA uses the =disko= and =sops-nix= inputs that nyx has now.

These blocks are not tangled.
They show the parts of =flake.nix= and =.sops.yaml= that LUNA needs.

The host entry in =nixosConfigurations=:

#+begin_src nix :tangle no
luna = mkHost {
  hostName = "luna";
  username = "coops";
  hardware = null;
  hostPath = ./hosts/luna;
  # The server has no home-manager modules. home-manager needs a stateVersion.
  homeModules = [ { home.stateVersion = "26.05"; } ];
};
#+end_src

The packages and the checks:

#+begin_src nix :tangle no
packages.${system} = {
  luna-core = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}.callPackage ./pkgs/luna-core { };
  luna-web = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}.callPackage ./pkgs/luna-web { };
  luna-core-ex = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}.callPackage ./pkgs/luna-core-ex { };
};
checks.${system} = {
  inherit (self.packages.${system}) luna-core luna-web luna-core-ex;
};
#+end_src

The sops rule for the LUNA secrets.
The rule must come before the general rule, because sops uses the first match.
The laptop host key (=&beta=) cannot read the LUNA secrets.

#+begin_src yaml :tangle no
keys:
  - &luna age15cjxnjrvt6l04dna9l2j25qfprfgwcxn3yds83uvjzwec3czefmsnmlx9n
creation_rules:
  - path_regex: secrets/luna\.yaml$
    key_groups:
      - age:
          - *coops
          - *luna
#+end_src

** Host configuration

mkHost in =flake.nix= sets the host name and adds the sops-nix module.
This host does not import the nyx desktop modules.
=modules/security.nix= is for the laptop: Secure Boot, NetworkManager, ClamAV.

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../hosts/luna/default.nix
{ lib, pkgs, modulesPath, inputs, ... }:
let
  site = import ./site.nix;
  secretsFile = ../../secrets/luna.yaml;
  secretsReady = builtins.pathExists secretsFile;
in
{
  imports = [
    (modulesPath + "/profiles/qemu-guest.nix")
    inputs.disko.nixosModules.disko
    ./disko.nix
    ../../modules/luna/hardening.nix
    ../../modules/luna/access.nix
    ../../modules/luna/matrix.nix
    ../../modules/luna/core.nix
    ../../modules/luna/web.nix
    ../../modules/luna/voice.nix
  ];

  _module.args = { inherit site secretsReady; };

  boot.loader.grub = {
    efiSupport = true;
    efiInstallAsRemovable = true;
  };
  boot.initrd.availableKernelModules = [
    "ahci" "xhci_pci" "virtio_pci" "virtio_scsi" "virtio_blk" "sd_mod" "sr_mod"
  ];

  networking.useDHCP = true;
  time.timeZone = site.timeZone;
  zramSwap.enable = true;

  # Without the secrets file, the configuration evaluates for checks only.
  # The install target refuses to start without the secrets file.
  sops = {
    defaultSopsFile =
      if secretsReady then secretsFile else "/run/luna-no-secrets.yaml";
    validateSopsFiles = secretsReady;
    age.sshKeyPaths = [ "/etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key" ];
  };
  warnings = lib.optional (!secretsReady)
    "secrets/luna.yaml does not exist. Run 'make secrets' before 'make install'.";

  nix.settings = {
    experimental-features = [ "nix-command" "flakes" ];
    allowed-users = [ "@wheel" ];
  };
  nix.gc = {
    automatic = true;
    dates = "weekly";
    options = "--delete-older-than 30d";
  };

  environment.systemPackages = with pkgs; [ git hledger htop ripgrep ];

  system.stateVersion = "26.05";
}
#+end_src

** Disk layout

The layout supports BIOS boot and UEFI boot.
Vultr can start the instance with either method.

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../hosts/luna/disko.nix
{
  disko.devices.disk.main = {
    type = "disk";
    device = "/dev/vda";
    content = {
      type = "gpt";
      partitions = {
        boot = {
          size = "1M";
          type = "EF02";
        };
        esp = {
          size = "512M";
          type = "EF00";
          content = {
            type = "filesystem";
            format = "vfat";
            mountpoint = "/boot";
            mountOptions = [ "umask=0077" ];
          };
        };
        root = {
          size = "100%";
          content = {
            type = "filesystem";
            format = "ext4";
            mountpoint = "/";
          };
        };
      };
    };
  };
}
#+end_src

** Hardening

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../modules/luna/hardening.nix
{ lib, site, ... }:
{
  boot.kernel.sysctl = {
    "kernel.kptr_restrict" = 2;
    "kernel.dmesg_restrict" = 1;
    "kernel.unprivileged_bpf_disabled" = 1;
    "net.ipv4.conf.all.accept_redirects" = 0;
    "net.ipv4.conf.all.send_redirects" = 0;
    "net.ipv6.conf.all.accept_redirects" = 0;
    "net.ipv4.tcp_syncookies" = 1;
  };

  security.protectKernelImage = true;
  environment.defaultPackages = lib.mkForce [ ];
  services.journald.settings.Journal.SystemMaxUse = "1G";

  # Port 22 is public only during the bootstrap.
  services.fail2ban.enable = site.bootstrap;
}
#+end_src

** Access: users, SSH and Tailscale

The secret name =coops-password= follows the nyx convention.
Root uses the same password, so the Vultr console rescue works.

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../modules/luna/access.nix
{ config, hostName, username, site, ... }:
let
  password = config.sops.secrets."${username}-password".path;
in
{
  users.mutableUsers = false;

  users.users.${username} = {
    isNormalUser = true;
    extraGroups = [ "wheel" "luna" ];
    openssh.authorizedKeys.keys = site.sshKeys;
    hashedPasswordFile = password;
  };
  users.users.root = {
    openssh.authorizedKeys.keys = site.sshKeys;
    hashedPasswordFile = password;
  };

  sops.secrets."${username}-password".neededForUsers = true;
  sops.secrets.tailscale-authkey = { };

  security.sudo.wheelNeedsPassword = true;

  # sshd runs during the bootstrap only. After the bootstrap, tailscaled
  # serves all SSH on the tailnet (Tailscale SSH). The Vultr console is the
  # way in when Tailscale fails.
  services.openssh = {
    enable = site.bootstrap;
    openFirewall = site.bootstrap;
    # The ed25519 key from `make host-key` is the only host key. Without this
    # line, NixOS also makes an RSA key, and sops-nix tries to read it as a
    # GPG key.
    hostKeys = [ { path = "/etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key"; type = "ed25519"; } ];
    settings = {
      PasswordAuthentication = false;
      KbdInteractiveAuthentication = false;
      PermitRootLogin = "prohibit-password";
    };
  };

  services.tailscale = {
    enable = true;
    authKeyFile = config.sops.secrets.tailscale-authkey.path;
    extraUpFlags = [ "--ssh" "--hostname=${hostName}" ];
  };

  networking.firewall = {
    enable = true;
    trustedInterfaces = [ "tailscale0" ];
    allowedUDPPorts = [ config.services.tailscale.port ];
  };
}
#+end_src

** Tailscale policy

Paste this policy in the Tailscale admin console, in place of the default policy.
The VPS has the tag =tag:luna=.
The policy lets your devices connect to each other and to the VPS.
The policy does not let the VPS connect to your devices.
The policy uses autogroups, so it does not depend on the login email address.

#+begin_src js :tangle tailscale/policy.hujson
// Tailscale policy for the nyx tailnet. The source is luna/LUNA.org.
{
    // Only an admin (you) can tag a device as luna.
    "tagOwners": {
        "tag:luna": ["autogroup:admin"],
    },

    "grants": [
        // Your devices can reach each other (laptop <-> phone).
        {"src": ["autogroup:member"], "dst": ["autogroup:self"], "ip": ["*"]},

        // Your devices can reach the VPS.
        {"src": ["autogroup:member"], "dst": ["tag:luna"], "ip": ["*"]},

        // Nothing lets tag:luna connect to your devices. If the VPS is
        // compromised, it cannot reach the laptop or the phone.
    ],

    "ssh": [
        // SSH between your own devices, with a re-login check.
        {
            "action": "check",
            "src":    ["autogroup:member"],
            "dst":    ["autogroup:self"],
            "users":  ["autogroup:nonroot", "root"],
        },
        // SSH from your devices to the VPS, as root or coops. This is
        // "accept", not "check", so make deploy does not stop for a
        // browser login.
        {
            "action": "accept",
            "src":    ["autogroup:member"],
            "dst":    ["tag:luna"],
            "users":  ["root", "coops"],
        },
    ],
}
#+end_src

** SSH client configuration

home-manager writes =~/.ssh/config= on the laptop.
The =luna= entry is in =home/agents.nix= in nyx, not in this file.
With the entry, =ssh luna= and the Makefile use the LUNA key.

#+begin_src nix :tangle no
"luna" = {
  hostname = "luna.bumblebee-tuatara.ts.net";
  identityFile = "~/.ssh/luna_ed25519";
  identitiesOnly = true;
  addKeysToAgent = "yes";
};
#+end_src

Before the install, Tailscale is not on the VPS.
=nixos-anywhere= uses the same key with the public IP address.
Add the public key to the instance in the Vultr dashboard.

** Matrix homeserver

Synapse listens on localhost only.
=tailscale serve= publishes Synapse on the tailnet with a valid HTTPS certificate.
Federation and registration are off.

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../modules/luna/matrix.nix
{ config, pkgs, hostName, site, ... }:
let
  fqdn = "${hostName}.${site.tailnet}";
  synapse = config.services.matrix-synapse.package;
  secretsConfig = config.sops.templates."synapse-secrets.yaml".path;
in
{
  services.matrix-synapse = {
    enable = true;
    settings = {
      server_name = fqdn;
      public_baseurl = "https://${fqdn}/";
      listeners = [
        {
          port = 8008;
          bind_addresses = [ "127.0.0.1" ];
          type = "http";
          tls = false;
          x_forwarded = true;
          resources = [ { names = [ "client" ]; compress = true; } ];
        }
      ];
      database.name = "sqlite3";
      # Room version 12 (MSC4291) makes room IDs with no server part.
      # ement.el refuses such a room ID. Room version 11 works with all
      # clients.
      default_room_version = "11";
      enable_registration = false;
      federation_domain_whitelist = [ ];
      allow_public_rooms_over_federation = false;
      trusted_key_servers = [ ];
      suppress_key_server_warning = true;
      url_preview_enabled = false;
      presence.enabled = false;
      report_stats = false;
    };
    extraConfigFiles = [ secretsConfig ];
  };

  sops.secrets.matrix-registration-secret = { };
  sops.templates."synapse-secrets.yaml" = {
    owner = "matrix-synapse";
    content = ''
      registration_shared_secret: "${config.sops.placeholder.matrix-registration-secret}"
    '';
  };

  systemd.services.luna-tailscale-serve = {
    description = "Publish the Matrix homeserver on the tailnet with HTTPS";
    after = [ "tailscaled.service" "matrix-synapse.service" ];
    wants = [ "tailscaled.service" ];
    wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
    serviceConfig = {
      Type = "oneshot";
      RemainAfterExit = true;
      Restart = "on-failure";
      RestartSec = 15;
    };
    script = ''
      ${config.services.tailscale.package}/bin/tailscale serve --bg --https=443 http://127.0.0.1:8008
      # The Phoenix app. Refer to "Build: the Phoenix app".
      ${config.services.tailscale.package}/bin/tailscale serve --bg --https=8443 http://127.0.0.1:8091
      # Stage E1 used port 8444 for the Python app. Delete that serve rule.
      ${config.services.tailscale.package}/bin/tailscale serve --https=8444 off || true
    '';
  };

  environment.systemPackages = [
    (pkgs.writeShellScriptBin "luna-register-user" ''
      exec ${synapse}/bin/register_new_matrix_user -c ${secretsConfig} "$@" http://127.0.0.1:8008
    '')
  ];
}
#+end_src

** luna-core service

The service runs as the system user =luna= in a strict systemd sandbox.
systemd gives the secrets to the service as credentials.
The memory hub is a bare git repository.
LUNA and the laptop both push to the hub.

#+begin_src nix :tangle ../modules/luna/core.nix
{ config, lib, pkgs, hostName, username, site, ... }:
let
  luna-core = pkgs.callPackage ../../pkgs/luna-core { };
  luna-core-ex = pkgs.callPackage ../../pkgs/luna-core-ex { };
  fqdn = "${hostName}.${site.tailnet}";
  stateDir = "/var/lib/luna";

  # The Elixir release needs a cookie, but it has no Erlang distribution, so
  # the cookie has no use on the network. The first start makes it.
  startElixir = pkgs.writeShellScript "luna-core-ex-start" ''
    set -euo pipefail
    umask 077
    if [ ! -s ${stateDir}/release-cookie ]; then
      ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > ${stateDir}/release-cookie
    fi
    RELEASE_COOKIE="$(cat ${stateDir}/release-cookie)"
    export RELEASE_COOKIE
    exec ${luna-core-ex}/bin/luna_core start
  '';
  secret = name: config.sops.secrets.${name}.path;
  credentials =
    [
      "anthropic_api_key:${secret "anthropic-api-key"}"
      "matrix_password:${secret "luna-matrix-password"}"
    ]
    ++ lib.optionals (lib.elem site.wise.mode [ "draft" "auto" ]) [
      "wise_api_token:${secret "wise-api-token"}"
    ]
    ++ lib.optionals site.telegram [
      "telegram_token:${secret "telegram-bot-token"}"
      "telegram_chat_id:${secret "telegram-chat-id"}"
    ];

  # The hub refuses raw bank exports. Raw exports go to the inbox, not to git.
  preReceive = pkgs.writeShellScript "luna-memory-pre-receive" ''
    set -euo pipefail
    export PATH=${lib.makeBinPath [ pkgs.git pkgs.coreutils pkgs.gnugrep ]}
    zero=0000000000000000000000000000000000000000
    status=0
    while read -r old new ref; do
      [ "$new" = "$zero" ] && continue
      if [ "$old" = "$zero" ]; then range="$new --not --all"; else range="$old..$new"; fi
      # shellcheck disable=SC2086
      bad=$(git log --name-only --diff-filter=AM --format= $range \
        | grep -iE '\.(csv|ofx|qif|qfx|xls|xlsx|pdf)$' || true)
      if [ -n "$bad" ]; then
        echo "LUNA: the memory refuses raw bank exports and statements:" >&2
        echo "$bad" | sed 's/^/  /' >&2
        echo "LUNA: send exports with 'make money-upload FILES=...'." >&2
        status=1
      fi
    done
    exit $status
  '';

  memoryInit = pkgs.writeShellScript "luna-memory-init" ''
    set -euo pipefail
    export PATH=${lib.makeBinPath [ pkgs.git pkgs.coreutils pkgs.findutils ]}
    export GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=LUNA GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL=luna@${fqdn}
    export GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=LUNA GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL=luna@${fqdn}
    hub=${stateDir}/memory.git
    work=${stateDir}/memory
    seed=${../../luna/memory-seed}
    if [ ! -d "$hub" ]; then
      git init --bare --shared=group --initial-branch=main "$hub"
    fi
    # Install the hook at each start, so the hook follows the configuration.
    cat ${preReceive} > "$hub/hooks/pre-receive"
    chmod 0775 "$hub/hooks/pre-receive"
    if [ ! -d "$work/.git" ]; then
      git clone "$hub" "$work"
    fi
    # The working copy has no commit. This is the first start, or a first
    # start that stopped before the commit.
    if ! git -C "$work" rev-parse --verify --quiet HEAD >/dev/null; then
      git -C "$work" symbolic-ref HEAD refs/heads/main
      if git -C "$hub" rev-parse --verify --quiet refs/heads/main >/dev/null; then
        # The hub has commits, for example from the laptop. Use them.
        git -C "$work" fetch origin
        git -C "$work" reset --hard origin/main
      else
        # Copy the seed without cp. cp sets the mode of each directory,
        # and RestrictSUIDSGID refuses a mode with the setgid bit. mkdir
        # and a redirect let the kernel set the inherited bits.
        (cd "$seed" && find . -type d) | while read -r d; do mkdir -p "$work/$d"; done
        (cd "$seed" && find . -type f) | while read -r f; do cat "$seed/$f" > "$work/$f"; done
        git -C "$work" add -A
        git -C "$work" commit -m "Add the memory seed files"
      fi
      git -C "$work" push --set-upstream origin main
    fi
    # Add seed files that a later version of LUNA brought. Never overwrite a
    # file: the memory belongs to the owner after the first start.
    if git -C "$work" pull --ff-only --quiet; then
      (cd "$seed" && find . -type f) | while read -r f; do
        if [ ! -e "$work/$f" ]; then
          mkdir -p "$work/$(dirname "$f")"
          cat "$seed/$f" > "$work/$f"
          git -C "$work" add -- "$f"
        fi
      done
      if ! git -C "$work" diff --cached --quiet; then
        git -C "$work" commit --quiet -m "Add new memory seed files"
        git -C "$work" push --quiet origin main
      fi
    fi
  '';
in
{
  users.groups.luna = { };
  # The clients of the core API. Refer to "Build: the core API".
  users.groups.luna-api = { };
  users.users.luna = {
    isSystemUser = true;
    group = "luna";
    home = stateDir;
  };

  sops.secrets = {
    anthropic-api-key = { };
    luna-matrix-password = { };
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (lib.elem site.wise.mode [ "draft" "auto" ]) {
    wise-api-token = { };
  } // lib.optionalAttrs site.telegram {
    telegram-bot-token = { };
    telegram-chat-id = { };
  };

  # Let the owner use the hub that the user "luna" owns.
  programs.git = {
    enable = true;
    config.safe.directory = [ "${stateDir}/memory.git" ];
  };

  systemd.tmpfiles.rules = [
    "d ${stateDir} 2750 luna luna -"
    "d ${stateDir}/approvals 0700 luna luna -"
    # The upload inbox for raw bank exports. The owner can write, luna reads.
    # The import deletes each file after the import.
    "d ${stateDir}/inbox 2770 luna luna -"
    "d ${stateDir}/audit 0750 luna luna -"
    "f ${stateDir}/audit/audit.jsonl 0640 luna luna -"
    "h ${stateDir}/audit/audit.jsonl - - - - +a"
  ];

  systemd.services.luna-core = {
    description = "LUNA core agent";
    after = [ "network-online.target" "matrix-synapse.service" ];
    wants = [ "network-online.target" ];
    wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
    path = [ pkgs.git pkgs.hledger ];
    environment = {
      LUNA_STATE_DIR = stateDir;
      LUNA_HOMESERVER = "http://127.0.0.1:8008";
      LUNA_MATRIX_USER = "@luna:${fqdn}";
      LUNA_OWNER = "@${username}:${fqdn}";
      LUNA_OWNER_TIMEZONE = site.ownerTimeZone;
      LUNA_BRIEF_TIME = site.briefTime;
      LUNA_MODEL = "claude-opus-5-5";
      LUNA_EFFORT = "medium";
      LUNA_MONEY = if site.money then "1" else "0";
      LUNA_WISE = builtins.toJSON site.wise;
      # The core API. Refer to "Build: the core API".
      LUNA_API_SOCKET = "/run/luna-api/api.sock";
      # The Elixir core: no Erlang distribution, and a writable temporary directory.
      RELEASE_DISTRIBUTION = "none";
      RELEASE_TMP = "/run/luna-core";
      GIT_AUTHOR_NAME = "LUNA";
      GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL = "luna@${fqdn}";
      GIT_COMMITTER_NAME = "LUNA";
      GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL = "luna@${fqdn}";
    };
    serviceConfig = {
      ExecStartPre = [
        # "+" runs this command as root, outside the sandbox. The setgid bit
        # gives the socket the group luna-api. Refer to "Access".
        "+${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/install -d -m 2750 -o luna -g luna-api /run/luna-api"
        memoryInit
      ];
      ExecStart = if site.core == "elixir" then startElixir else "${luna-core}/bin/luna-core";
      RuntimeDirectory = "luna-core";
      RuntimeDirectoryMode = "0700";
      User = "luna";
      Group = "luna";
      UMask = "0007";
      LoadCredential = credentials;
      # Wait longer after each failure: 30 s, then up to 10 min. A fast
      # restart loop makes many failed Matrix logins, and Synapse then
      # refuses all logins for some minutes.
      Restart = "on-failure";
      RestartSec = 30;
      RestartSteps = 5;
      RestartMaxDelaySec = 600;

      # "-": the path can be absent. systemd sets up this namespace for the
      # "+" command too, and that command makes /run/luna-api.
      ReadWritePaths = [ stateDir "-/run/luna-api" ];
      ProtectSystem = "strict";
      ProtectHome = true;
      PrivateTmp = true;
      PrivateDevices = true;
      NoNewPrivileges = true;
      CapabilityBoundingSet = "";
      ProtectKernelTunables = true;
      ProtectKernelModules = true;
      ProtectKernelLogs = true;
      ProtectControlGroups = true;
      ProtectClock = true;
      ProtectHostname = true;
      ProtectProc = "invisible";
      RestrictAddressFamilies = [ "AF_INET" "AF_INET6" "AF_UNIX" ];
      RestrictNamespaces = true;
      RestrictRealtime = true;
      RestrictSUIDSGID = true;
      LockPersonality = true;
      SystemCallArchitectures = "native";
      SystemCallFilter = [ "@system-service" "~@privileged" ];
    };
  };
}
#+end_src
